2.6 Integrator

An op-amp circuit that performs mathematical integration of the input signal. A capacitor replaces Rf in the feedback path.

ğŸ“ Derivation â€” Integrator Output Equation
Step 1: Virtual ground â†’ Vâˆ’ â‰ˆ 0V
Step 2: Current through R:   I = Vin / R
Step 3: This current charges capacitor C. Voltage across C = (1/C)âˆ«I dt
Step 4: Vout = âˆ’Vc = âˆ’(1/C)âˆ«I dt = âˆ’(1/RC)âˆ«Vin dt
Result: Vout = âˆ’(1/RC) âˆ« Vin dt

For a square wave input â†’ integrator output is a triangular wave. For a sine wave input â†’ output is cosine (phase shifted).

Integrator: capacitor feedback-àµ½. Square wave input â†’ triangular wave output. Vout = âˆ’(1/RC)âˆ«Vin dt. RC value time constant control à´šàµ†à´¯àµà´¯àµà´¨àµà´¨àµ.

2.7 Differentiator

Performs mathematical differentiation. Capacitor is in the input path (replaces R), and R is in the feedback path.

ğŸ“ Derivation â€” Differentiator Output Equation
Step 1: Current through capacitor C:   I = C Ã— d(Vin)/dt
Step 2: Virtual ground â†’ Vâˆ’ = 0, so this current flows through Rf
Step 3: Vout = âˆ’I Ã— Rf = âˆ’Rf Ã— C Ã— d(Vin)/dt
Result: Vout = âˆ’RC Ã— dVin/dt

For a triangular wave input â†’ differentiator output is a square wave. For a sine wave â†’ output is cosine.

Differentiator: input-àµ½ capacitor. Triangular wave â†’ square wave output. Vout = âˆ’RC Ã— dVin/dt. Input change rate à´†à´£àµ output determine à´šàµ†à´¯àµà´¯àµà´¨àµà´¨à´¤àµ.

2.8 Comparators

Comparator

An op-amp circuit (usually open-loop) that compares two voltages and produces a binary output â€” either +Vsat or âˆ’Vsat â€” depending on which input is larger.

Comparator: 2 voltages compare à´šàµ†à´¯àµà´¯àµà´¨àµà´¨ circuit. V+ > Vâˆ’ à´†à´£àµ†à´™àµà´•à´¿àµ½ output = +Vsat. V+ < Vâˆ’ à´†à´£àµ†à´™àµà´•à´¿àµ½ output = âˆ’Vsat. Binary (two-state) output.

Positive Voltage Level Detector

  • Reference voltage Vref (positive) applied to non-inverting (+) terminal
  • Input Vin applied to inverting (âˆ’) terminal
  • When Vin < Vref â†’ Vout = +Vsat
  • When Vin > Vref â†’ Vout = âˆ’Vsat
  • Detects when input exceeds a positive reference level

Negative Voltage Level Detector

  • Reference voltage Vref (negative) applied to inverting (âˆ’) terminal
  • When Vin > Vref â†’ Vout = +Vsat
  • When Vin < Vref â†’ Vout = âˆ’Vsat

Zero Crossing Detector

A special case of comparator where Vref = 0V. The output switches polarity whenever the input crosses 0V.

  • Non-inverting input connected to GND (Vref = 0)
  • When Vin > 0 â†’ Vout = âˆ’Vsat
  • When Vin < 0 â†’ Vout = +Vsat
  • Converts a sine wave to a square wave
Zero crossing detector: Input 0V cross à´šàµ†à´¯àµà´¯àµà´®àµà´ªàµ‹àµ¾ output switch à´†à´•àµà´‚. Sine wave input â†’ Square wave output. Vref = 0V.

2.9 Precision Rectifier (Half-Wave)

A normal diode has a forward voltage drop of ~0.6â€“0.7V. For small signals (below 0.7V), a regular diode rectifier fails. A precision rectifier uses an op-amp to eliminate this drop â€” it can rectify even millivolt signals.

Normal diode rectifier-à´¨àµ 0.7V forward voltage drop à´‰à´£àµà´Ÿàµ. Small signals rectify à´šàµ†à´¯àµà´¯à´¾àµ» à´•à´´à´¿à´¯à´¿à´²àµà´². Precision rectifier-àµ½ op-amp à´‰à´ªà´¯àµ‹à´—à´¿à´•àµà´•àµà´¨àµà´¨àµ â€” 0.7V drop à´‡à´²àµà´²à´¾à´¤àµ† exact rectification à´šàµ†à´¯àµà´¯àµà´‚.
  • Diode D1 is in the feedback loop of op-amp
  • During positive half: D1 conducts â†’ output = input (no diode drop)
  • During negative half: D1 reverse biased â†’ op-amp output goes to âˆ’Vsat, Vout = 0

Module 2 â€” Quick Check Questions

1. What is virtual ground? â–¶
In an inverting op-amp with negative feedback, the inverting input (Vâˆ’) is held at approximately 0V by the feedback, even though it is not connected to ground. This is virtual ground. It simplifies circuit analysis.
2. Derive the gain of an inverting amplifier. â–¶
Using virtual ground (Vâˆ’ = 0): Current through Rin = Vin/Rin. This current flows through Rf (op-amp draws no current). Vout = 0 âˆ’ (Vin/Rin)Ã—Rf = âˆ’(Rf/Rin)Ã—Vin. Gain Av = âˆ’Rf/Rin.
3. What is the output of an integrator for a square wave input? â–¶
A triangular wave. During the positive half of square wave, capacitor charges linearly â†’ output is a negative ramp. During negative half â†’ positive ramp. Combined result = triangular wave.
4. Why is a precision rectifier needed? â–¶
A normal diode has ~0.7V forward bias drop. For signals below 0.7V, rectification fails. An op-amp precision rectifier eliminates this drop using the feedback loop, allowing accurate rectification of even millivolt-level signals.
5. Compare CMRR of ideal and practical op-amp. â–¶
Ideal op-amp: CMRR = âˆ (perfect common-mode rejection). Practical IC 741: CMRR â‰ˆ 90 dB. Higher CMRR means better ability to reject noise and interference that appears equally on both inputs.

3.1 Number Systems

3.4 Negative Number Representation: Complements

One's Complement

Invert all bits: 0 becomes 1, 1 becomes 0.

Example: One's complement of (1010)â‚‚
Invert all bits: 1â†’0, 0â†’1, 1â†’0, 0â†’1

Two's Complement

Two's Complement Formula
Two's complement = One's complement + 1
Example: Two's complement of (1010)â‚‚
One's complement of 1010 = 0101
Add 1: 0101 + 0001 = 0110
Two's complement = 0110

Binary Subtraction Using Two's Complement

ğŸ”¢ Subtract: (1010)â‚‚ âˆ’ (0110)â‚‚ using two's complement
Step 1: Find two's complement of subtrahend (0110): One's comp = 1001, + 1 = 1010
Step 2: Add: 1010 + 1010 = 10100
Step 3: Discard carry (MSB beyond 4 bits): 0100
Result = 0100 = (4)â‚â‚€ âœ“ (10âˆ’6=4)
Two's complement subtraction: subtrahend-à´¨àµà´±àµ† two's complement à´•à´£àµà´Ÿàµà´ªà´¿à´Ÿà´¿à´•àµà´•àµ‚, à´ªà´¿à´¨àµà´¨àµ† add à´šàµ†à´¯àµà´¯àµ‚. Carry discard à´šàµ†à´¯àµà´¯àµ‚. Result = difference.

3.5 Logic Gates

Logic Gate

A basic electronic circuit that performs a logical operation on one or more binary inputs to produce a single binary output. Building blocks of all digital circuits.

AND Gate

Operation: Output is 1 only when ALL inputs are 1.

Boolean Expression: Y = A Â· B or Y = AB

ABY=AB
000
010
100
111

OR Gate

Operation: Output is 1 when ANY input is 1.

Boolean Expression: Y = A + B

ABY=A+B
000
011
101
111

NOT Gate (Inverter)

Operation: Output is complement of input.

Boolean Expression: Y = A' or Y = Ä€

AY=A'
01
10

NAND Gate (Universal Gate)

Operation: NOT-AND. Output is 0 only when ALL inputs are 1.

Boolean Expression: Y = (AB)'

Universal gate: can implement any logic function using only NAND gates.

ABY=(AB)'
001
011
101
110

NOR Gate (Universal Gate)

Operation: NOT-OR. Output is 1 only when ALL inputs are 0.

Boolean Expression: Y = (A+B)'

Universal gate: any logic function can be built using only NOR gates.

ABY=(A+B)'
001
010
100
110

XOR Gate (Exclusive OR)

Operation: Output is 1 when inputs are different.

Boolean Expression: Y = A âŠ• B = A'B + AB'

Used in half adder, parity checker, comparator circuits.

ABY=AâŠ•B
000
011
101
110
XOR gate: inputs different â†’ output 1. Inputs same â†’ output 0. Half adder-àµ½ sum calculate à´šàµ†à´¯àµà´¯à´¾àµ» use à´šàµ†à´¯àµà´¯àµà´¨àµà´¨àµ. NAND and NOR = universal gates (à´‡à´µ à´’à´¨àµà´¨àµ à´®à´¤à´¿, à´¬à´¾à´•àµà´•à´¿ gates build à´šàµ†à´¯àµà´¯à´¾à´‚).

3.6 Boolean Algebra

Laws and Rules of Boolean Algebra (statements):

Law / RuleAND FormOR Form
Identity LawA Â· 1 = AA + 0 = A
Null (Annulment)A Â· 0 = 0A + 1 = 1
IdempotentA Â· A = AA + A = A
ComplementA Â· A' = 0A + A' = 1
Double Complement(A')' = A
CommutativeA Â· B = B Â· AA + B = B + A
Associative(AÂ·B)Â·C = AÂ·(BÂ·C)(A+B)+C = A+(B+C)
DistributiveAÂ·(B+C) = AB+ACA+(BÂ·C) = (A+B)Â·(A+C)
AbsorptionAÂ·(A+B) = AA+AB = A

De Morgan's Theorems

Theorem 1: The complement of a product is the sum of complements.

(A Â· B)' = A' + B'

Theorem 2: The complement of a sum is the product of complements.

(A + B)' = A' Â· B'

De Morgan's is used to: break a NAND/NOR expression, simplify complex Boolean expressions, convert between SOP and POS forms.

De Morgan's: AND-à´¨àµà´±àµ† complement = OR of complements. OR-à´¨àµà´±àµ† complement = AND of complements. NAND â†” OR with inverters. NOR â†” AND with inverters.

3.7 Standard Forms: SOP and POS

SOP (Sum of Products)

A Boolean expression where product terms (ANDs) are summed (ORed). Each product term contains all variables in either true or complemented form.
